## 2. DISAGREEMENTS -- please adjudicate your own side

**D1 -- what the new lane does when build/ has no objects.**  Today: the pristine arm cannot link, the helper says
SETUP, the gate row REDS -- while the #442 main lane in the same file `note`s "SKIPPED, not passed" and adds no check.

* **kimi:** "SETUP-from-absent-`build/` is an environment state ... A red on a machine without `build/` is noise ... test
  the object list for emptiness up front -> empty => NOTE-skip mirroring the main lane."
* **grok:** "Skip-when-unbuilt is the one I would still take."  **glm:** "Mirror the NOTE, assert build/ once for both, or
  make both red -- pick one and record it."
* **agy:** "This asymmetry is right: selfmutants operate under a fail-closed doctrine ... gate 2 runs after `gate_build`
  in `run.sh`, so `build/` objects are always present during battery runs."
* **codex:** "Do not copy the note-only skip.  ... the later `SELFCHECK manifest: every COVERED detector ran its
  sentinel` also requires `diff_fbpending_bound.log`.  An absent log fails; a retained previous log can satisfy it
  because the no-object branch does not regenerate it.  If buildless operation is supported, use the existing
  `degrade()` mechanism ... and coordinate both lanes and sentinel accounting."
* minimax: a defensible trade-off; deepseek: a policy question.
* **Opus (MEASURED with lib.sh):** a gate whose only missing piece is `note`d ends PASS rc 0; with `degrade` it ends SKIP
  rc 77.  "Today the new lane's red is the ONLY thing stopping gate 2 from reporting PASS when build/ has no objects."
  Right fix: `degrade` in BOTH lanes and truncate the log before the main lane.

**My ruling: KEEP RED this round, and FILE `fbnobuild`** -- gate 2's whole buildless behaviour as ONE explicit `degrade()`
SKIP across the #442 lane, this lane and the SC sentinel accounting.  Warrant: codex's finding means copying the NOTE
would add a SECOND path by which a buildless gate 2 reports green (the #442 lane already has one, through a stale log),
while red is loud and unreachable in the battery; the right fix touches #442's lane, which is not this round's.
**kimi, grok, glm: is that warrant sound, or is red-on-bare-host the worse harm?**

**D2 -- the option set** (every seat named members).  Widen `selfmutant.py`'s token grammar; print both tokens; stagger
the eleven; retire an undischargeable probe; reclassify detector -> witness.  **The owner's rulings stand** (fix
#537 rather than re-date; wire two and re-date eleven as one block).  Recorded, not re-litigated -- but if a member
is WRONG to reject for a reason the rulings did not weigh, say so.

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

1637:
1638:#  A PRESERVED LOG IS VERIFIED BY ITS COMPLETION SENTINEL, NOT BY ITS EXISTENCE.  The probe
1639:#  gained PIT8253_LATCH_DONE specifically so this row could be written correctly the first
1640:#  time -- before it, the last line was a bare "PASS", a word that occurs in dozens of other
1641:#  contexts and cannot distinguish a COMPLETED run from a TRUNCATED one.
1642:if ! grep -q "PIT8253_LATCH_DONE" "$PITLOG"; then
1643:    note "pit8253 probe did not reach its completion sentinel; last lines follow"
1644:    tail -5 "$PITLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
1645:    check "pit8253 probe completed" "no" "yes"
1646:else
1647:    grep -E "^  (ok|FAIL) " "$PITLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
1648:
1649:    #  THE THREE CONTROLS PLUS THE MMU-ON NEGATIVE CONTROL, each pinned by name.  A count of
1650:    #  green rows would let a control go red while the total stayed acceptable.
1651:    for c in "C1 liveness" "C2 MMU state" "A1 device signature" "T  MMU-ON control"; do
1652:        check "pit8253 control: $c" \
1653:              "$(grep -c "^  ok   $c" "$PITLOG")" "1"
1654:    done
1655:
1656:    #  Per-row presence.  A row that silently stops being tested is the padded-column class,
1657:    #  so each is checked individually rather than by a total.  Eighteen rows: 4 controls +
1658:    #  E/A2 apparatus + R0..R11.
1659:    for v in "E  encodings" "A2 recompute stream" \
1660:             "R0 the run produced data" "R1 latch preserves programming" \
1661:             "R2 latch is not an error" "R3 latched read is not the reload" \
1662:             "R4 16-bit readback is the reload" "R5 one recompute per write pair" \
1663:             "R6 no transient count of zero" \
1664:             "R7 read and write flip-flops are separate" \
1665:             "R8 the latch is released by reading it" \
1666:             "R9 a mid-pair latch completes the pair and lets go" \
1667:             "R10 a Control Word rewinds the write flip-flop" \
1668:             "R11 ...and it does so PER COUNTER, not just counter 0"; do
1669:        check "pit8253 row green: $v" \
1670:              "$(grep -c "^  ok   $v" "$PITLOG")" "1"
1671:    done
1672:
1673:    #  The denominator against a LITERAL: a probe that loses rows cannot pass by shrinking.
1674:    check "pit8253 rows run" "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) ' "$PITLOG")" "18"
1675:fi

1:#!/bin/bash
2:# GATE 2 -- offline differential of the REAL ieee_store_float_value().
3:#
4:# The strongest gate here, and the cheapest, but only because it links the shipped
5:# src/core/float_emul.c. The first version of this gate transcribed BOTH sides of the
6:# differential and compared the copy against itself -- it never compiled or executed
7:# float_emul.c at all, so deleting #287 from the shipped source left it green. Review
8:# caught that, not the gate. Two things now prevent a repeat:
9:#
10:#   * the real file is compiled and linked into the driver, and
11:#   * the file compiled is asserted byte-identical to the committed one, so "the test
12:#     passed" and "the repository is correct" are the same statement.
13:#
14:# misc.h includes ../../config.h, which configure generates, so the compile has to happen
15:# against a CONFIGURED tree. That tree is a copy of the committed source (no VPATH in this
16:# project), which is exactly why the identity check above is required rather than assumed.
17:set -u
18:HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
19:. "$HERE/lib.sh"

26:# #375: the mips*_loadstore[32] dispatch-table index is a SILENT CROSS-FILE
27:# COUPLING. generate_mips_loadstore.c emits the table in the loop order
28:# `endianness -> store -> size -> signedness`, so an entry's index is
29:# endianness*16 + store*8 + size*2 + signedness. That arithmetic is then
30:# hard-coded BY HAND, with no comment and no check, at a dozen sites: the
31:# multi-transfer fold's bail (`mips32_loadstore[5]` = plain lw), the coproc
32:# handlers (lwc1->5, swc1->12, ldc1->7, sdc1->14), the canonical decoder, and
33:# the COMBINE(nop)/strlen/#169 matcher slots. Reorder that one generator loop
34:# and every hand-coded index silently mis-resolves -- the fold bail dispatches
35:# the wrong access SIZE, ldc1 becomes lw -- and NOTHING fails to compile. These
36:# rows turn the coupling into a checked invariant, offline, needing no compiler
37:# and no rig (only the committed generated table). Found by the #46 MIPS-combiner
38:# audit; both boot rigs are LE, so the _be entries are dead code on the harness
39:# but the coupling is real for any future BE MIPS work.
40:#
41:# The byte rows are load-bearing in the opposite direction: size==0 has no
42:# endianness, so the generator makes the byte LE and BE entries the SAME symbol
43:# ([1]==[17], [8]==[24]). #169's byte-store check and the strlen byte-load check
44:# rely on that share; the word rows ([5]!=[21]) prove the discrimination the byte
45:# rows deliberately lack. If a generator change ever split the byte entries, the
46:# `|| [x+16]` matcher tautologies become live and those checks silently weaken.
47:MIPSLS=$SEC/src/cpus/tmp_mips_loadstore.c
48:need_file "$MIPSLS"
49:
50:#  ls_entry <array-name> <index> -> the function symbol at that index, read from
51:#  the committed generated table (the [32] arrays, not the [16] generic ones).
52:ls_entry() {
53:    awk -v arr="$1" -v idx="$2" '
54:        $0 ~ ("\\*" arr "\\[32\\]") { f = 1; n = 0; next }
55:        f && /^};/ { f = 0 }
56:        f { gsub(/[ \t,};]/, "");
57:            if ($0 != "") { if (n == idx) { print $0; exit } n++ } }
58:    ' "$MIPSLS"
59:}
60:
61:for arr in mips32_loadstore mips_loadstore; do
62:    pfx=${arr%_loadstore}
63:    check "  $arr[5]  = plain word-load LE"  "$(ls_entry $arr 5)"  "${pfx}_instr_l4_le"
64:    check "  $arr[12] = plain word-store LE" "$(ls_entry $arr 12)" "${pfx}_instr_s4_le"
65:    check "  $arr[21] = plain word-load BE"  "$(ls_entry $arr 21)" "${pfx}_instr_l4_be"
66:    check "  $arr byte-load LE/BE share [1]==[17]" \
67:        "$([ "$(ls_entry $arr 1)" = "$(ls_entry $arr 17)" ] && echo same || echo differ)" "same"
68:    check "  $arr byte-store LE/BE share [8]==[24]" \
69:        "$([ "$(ls_entry $arr 8)" = "$(ls_entry $arr 24)" ] && echo same || echo differ)" "same"
70:    check "  $arr word-load LE != BE [5]!=[21]" \
835:    #  purge-asserting row ran with both selectors at 0 because fresh() memsets dev.  A
836:    #  pass-2 measure seat drove FIVE REAL DEFECTS through that gap, the smallest being one
837:    #  appended `&& d->cmmu_nr == 0`.  MEASURED: the data CMMU takes 51.9% of all SAR writes
838:    #  per boot, and a witness driving SAR through the handler shows the WRONG PAGE flushed
839:    #  and a stale supervisor translation kept.
840:    #
841:    #  WHEN A ROUND SPLITS AN ARM, EVERY ROW THAT PINNED A PROPERTY OF THE OLD ARM NOW PINS
842:    #  IT FOR ONLY ONE HALF.  Re-pinning the rows that go RED is the obvious half; the rows
843:    #  that stay GREEN while quietly covering less are the half that gets missed.
844:    check "m8invread: the split-arm selector rows are present" \
845:          "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) +(G6|G7|G8|G9) ' "$IVLOG")" "4"
846:    check "m8invread: the same-value-write row is present"           "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) +(F4) ' "$IVLOG")" "1"
847:    #  The neighbouring arm, ~603,000 purges/boot: nothing above would notice its loss.
848:    check "m8invread: the SCR-arm control rows are present"           "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) +(H1|H2) ' "$IVLOG")" "2"
849:    check "m8invread: identity row present"           "$(grep -c 'IDENTITY row count' "$IVLOG")" "1"
850:fi

853:#  CAN THESE DETECTORS STILL FAIL?  (#436)
854:#
855:#  Gate 3 is the battery's only failability control and it mutates ONE file,
856:#  src/core/float_emul.c.  Eleven differentials live here; gate 3 names one.  A
857:#  measuring seat reproduced the consequence: stub check() so it never compares,
858:#  and 118 rows across five detectors print `ok`, keep their row counts, keep
859:#  their identity rows, keep their verdict tokens -- and detect NOTHING, with
860:#  every assertion in this file green.
861:#
862:#  The reason was structural, not an oversight in any one row: every assertion in
863:#  this file grepped the detector's own stdout, and all of that stdout is downstream
864:#  of the single comparison the stub removes.  They were ONE EQUIVALENCE CLASS, and
865:#  the stub stepped over all of it at once.
866:#
867:#  PAST TENSE ON PURPOSE.  A pass-2 seat pointed out that this sentence was written
868:#  in the PRESENT tense by the very hunk that falsified it: the five selfmutant_one
869:#  checks grep the HELPER's output (varied input) and the manifest checks grep the
870:#  FILESYSTEM, so a handful of this gate's assertions are now outside the class.
871:#  That is the whole point of adding them, and stating it as a present fact would
872:#  have made the comment wrong the moment it landed.  Only a check that VARIES THE INPUT and
873:#  demands the output track it escapes.
874:#
875:#  TWO CONTROLS, COVERING DIFFERENT FAILURES, NEITHER SUBSTITUTING FOR THE OTHER:
876:#    * the in-detector SELFCHECK row (in the .c files) -- feeds check() a
877:#      mismatch and requires the failure counter to move.  Covers EVERY row in
878:#      its file against the comparator dying.  Free, no extra compile.
879:#    * the self-mutants below -- break the code the detector watches and require
880:#      the NAMED row to catch it.  Covers ONE site against a fixture that has
881:#      stopped reaching the code.  Measured: the SELFCHECK is blind to a row
882:#      rewritten to compare the subject against itself; a pinned mutant is not.
883:#
884:#  WHAT THIS DOES NOT PROVE, said plainly because the honest scope is the whole
885:#  point.  A seat stubbed every row EXCEPT the ones each mutant kills and both
886:#  this gate and the self-mutant stayed green -- 23 of 24 rows inert for timer,
887:#  25 of 27 for m8invread.  So this is a LIVENESS CONTROL: it proves the detector
888:#  is not wholly inert and that one named row still fires.  It is not a census
889:#  and must never be read as "the detector is fine".  Going from 0 to 1 is not
890:#  false comfort; CALLING 1 "the detector works" is.
891:selfmutant_one() {   # harness subject stem row-id [extra-cc-flags]
892:    local h="$1" subj="$2" stem="$3" row="$4" xflags="${5:-}"
893:    local o="$HERE/selfmutants/$stem.old" n="$HERE/selfmutants/$stem.new"
894:    if [ ! -f "$o" ] || [ ! -f "$n" ]; then
895:        check "selfmutant $stem: fragments present" "no" "yes"; return
896:    fi
897:    local out
898:    #  The extra flags are the caller's, because the caller owns the real compile line.
899:    #  memory_rw needs -fno-optimize-sibling-calls, which THIS FILE calls load-bearing for
900:    #  the loop-not-recursion row; the helper hardcoded one flag set and so compiled that
901:    #  harness differently from the battery, which the pristine control arm cannot detect
902:    #  because both arms are then consistently wrong.
903:    out=$(SELFMUTANT_EXTRA_FLAGS="$xflags"           python3 "$HERE/selfmutant.py" "$h" "$subj" "$o" "$n" "$row" 2>&1)
904:    printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -E '^       ' | sed 's/^/    /'
905:    #  ASSERT THE OK TOKEN IS PRESENT, never "FAIL is absent": a setup error emits
906:    #  neither, so a negative form is satisfied by a control that never ran.
907:    check "selfmutant $stem: the detector still detects, via its named row" \
908:          "$(printf '%s' "$out" | grep -c 'SELFMUTANT_OK')" "1"
909:}

1133:#  "6, shrinking not growing" -- and a seat measured that SHRINKING IT IS RED (got=5 want=6),
1134:#  so the pin actively obstructed the thing its own name claimed to want, and the name embedded
1135:#  the constant so a legitimate shrink would print "the exemption list is 6 ...   5".  A count
1136:#  pins; a date expires.  Shrinking is now green by construction: there is nothing to retune.
1137:#  *** A MALFORMED EXEMPTION DATE NEVER EXPIRED, AND IT STILL SATISFIED THE COVERAGE CHECK.
1138:#  ***  REPRODUCED against the shipped loop, with both controls green (a well-formed past date
1139:#  DID expire; a well-formed future one did not):
1140:#
1141:#      ieee_store            no date at all   -> never expires
1142:#      ieee_store:2026-8-1   unpadded, 19 days past -> never expires
1143:#      ieee_store:garbage    not a date       -> never expires
1144:#
1145:#  MECHANISM.  `${e##*:}` strips to the LAST colon -- and with NO colon it strips nothing and
1146:#  returns the whole entry, so `ieee_store` was compared as a date: 's' > '2', false, unexpired.
1147:#  The `""` arm only catches `stem:` with an empty tail, which is the one malformed case that
1148:#  was handled.  For the unpadded form the compare is worse than useless: `2026-8-1` against
1149:#  `2026-08-20` matches through `2026-`, then '8' > '0', so a date 19 days PAST sorts as
1150:#  FUTURE.  And the coverage check keys on `${e%%:*}` -- the stem -- so the exemption is
1151:#  honoured throughout.  The teeth of the exempt arm depended on a format NOTHING VALIDATED.
1152:#
1153:#  THE FIX IS TO REFUSE, NOT TO GUESS.  A malformed entry is now RED and names itself; it is
1154:#  never silently treated as unexpired.  A `case` glob rather than a regex, because this runs
1155:#  under POSIX sh and `[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]` needs no external process.
1156:#
1157:#  ONE HELPER FOR BOTH MANIFESTS.  SM_EXEMPT and SC_EXEMPT carried byte-identical loops a few
1158:#  hundred lines apart, so a fix to one would have left the other -- which is this project's

1:#!/usr/bin/env python3
2:"""CAN THIS DETECTOR STILL FAIL WHEN THE CODE IT WATCHES IS BROKEN?
3:
4:Usage:
5:    selfmutant.py <harness.c> <subject-relpath> <old-fragment-file> <new-fragment-file> <row-id>
6:
7:Prints a small report and one of:
8:    SELFMUTANT_OK        the mutant was applied, the detector went FAIL, and the named row
9:                         was among the kills, and the PRISTINE build through the same tree
10:                         still PASSed
11:    SELFMUTANT_FAIL      the detector did NOT detect the mutant
12:    SELFMUTANT_SETUP     something about the apparatus is wrong -- anchor missing or not
13:                         unique, build failed, no verdict token, the control arm failed.
14:                         NEVER scored as a detection.
15:
16:Exit 0 only for SELFMUTANT_OK.
17:
18:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19:WHY THIS EXISTS.  A measuring seat reproduced the defect: stub check() so it never compares,
20:and 118 rows across five detectors print `ok`, keep their row counts and identity rows and
21:verdict tokens, and detect NOTHING -- with every gate-2 assertion green.  Every one of those
22:assertions greps the detector's own stdout, and all of that stdout is downstream of the one
23:comparison the stub removes.  They are ONE EQUIVALENCE CLASS.  Only a check that VARIES THE
24:INPUT and demands the output track it escapes.
25:
26:The in-detector SELFCHECK row covers the comparator dying (all rows at once).  THIS covers a
27:different failure: the fixture no longer reaching the code, so a row is live but blind.
28:Measured as complementary -- neither substitutes for the other.
29:
30:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31:FOUR THINGS THIS GETS RIGHT THAT THE OBVIOUS VERSION GETS WRONG, each measured by a seat:
32:
33:1.  ASSERT THE FAIL TOKEN IS PRESENT, never "the PASS token is absent".  A build failure
34:    emits NEITHER token, so the negative form is satisfied by a control that never ran.
35:
36:2.  PIN THE KILL TO THE NAMED ROW.  A verdict-only assertion is VACUOUS on 4 of 5 harnesses:
37:    stub only the row this mutant was chosen to certify, and other rows still kill the
38:    mutant, so the control stays green while the row it vouches for has stopped detecting.
39:
40:3.  THE ANCHOR MUST BE UNIQUE, not merely present.  gate 3's applier tests membership and
41:    then replaces the first occurrence, so a future duplication silently mutates the wrong
42:    site -- and a wrong-site mutant that survives looks exactly like a real survivor.  That
43:    trap has already cost this project a false result once.  count() != 1 is SETUP, not FAIL.
44:
45:4.  THE DETECTOR REACHES ITS SUBJECT BY A QUOTED RELATIVE INCLUDE (`#include
46:    "../src/devices/dev_m8820x.c"`), which resolves against the INCLUDING FILE's directory
47:    first -- so no -I redirection can substitute a mutant.  The mutant tree must contain the
48:    detector too.  Layout below: everything symlinked pristine except the one real mutated
49:    file, so the lane compiles what the battery compiles apart from the single edit.
50:"""
51:import hashlib
52:import io
53:import os
54:import re
55:import shutil
56:import signal
57:import subprocess
58:import sys
59:import tempfile

62:SEC = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(HERE, ".."))
63:
64:#  The same flags gate 2 uses.  -Wl,--gc-sections is load-bearing for several of these
65:#  (without it the link needs memory_device_register); -lm is harmless.
66:CC = ("gcc -O2 -std=c99 -D_POSIX_C_SOURCE=200809L -D_DEFAULT_SOURCE "
67:      "-I../src -I../src/include -I../src/include/thirdparty "
68:      "-ffunction-sections -fdata-sections -Wl,--gc-sections")
69:
70:#  *** PER-HARNESS FLAGS, BECAUSE ONE HARDCODED SET IS A LANE THAT COMPILES SOMETHING
71:#  ELSE. ***  gate 2 builds diff_memory_rw.c with -fno-optimize-sibling-calls, and its own
72:#  comment calls that LOAD-BEARING: gcc 15.2.1 eliminated the tail call and the
73:#  loop-not-recursion row passed under the very defect it exists for.  This helper hardcoded
74:#  a single CC line without it, so for that harness the self-mutant lane compiled DIFFERENTLY
75:#  from the battery.
76:#
77:#  The pristine control arm cannot catch that: both arms are consistently wrong, so it agrees
78:#  with itself.  A control that compares two copies of the same mistake is the shape this
79:#  whole round exists to remove.  A reading seat named it; the flag now comes in as an
80:#  argument so the caller -- which owns the real compile line -- supplies it.
81:EXTRA_FLAGS = os.environ.get("SELFMUTANT_EXTRA_FLAGS", "")

84:def out(tag, *msg):
85:    print("       %-9s %s" % (tag, " ".join(str(m) for m in msg)))

88:def link_tree(src, dst, real_file=None, real_text=None):
89:    """Mirror `src` into `dst` with symlinks, except one file written for real."""
90:    os.makedirs(dst, exist_ok=True)
91:    for name in os.listdir(src):
92:        s, d = os.path.join(src, name), os.path.join(dst, name)
93:        if real_file and os.path.abspath(s) == os.path.abspath(real_file):
94:            io_write(d, real_text)
95:        elif os.path.isdir(s):
96:            if real_file and os.path.abspath(real_file).startswith(os.path.abspath(s) + os.sep):
97:                link_tree(s, d, real_file, real_text)
98:            else:
99:                os.symlink(s, d)
100:        else:
101:            os.symlink(s, d)

103:
104:def io_write(path, text):
105:    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
106:        f.write(text)
107:
108:
109:#  *** TIMEOUTS, AND THEY ARE NOT SHARED.  A HANG PRODUCED NO TOKEN AT ALL. ***
110:#  Neither subprocess call had a timeout, so a mutant that loops forever emitted NOTHING --
111:#  not OK, not FAIL, not SETUP -- and gate 2 wedged with nothing to grade.  Reproduced: an
112:#  outer `timeout 45` had to fire from outside; the helper never returned.
113:#
114:#  The two phases get SEPARATE budgets because they mean different things.  A two-minute
115:#  compile means the HOST is sick; a spinning run is an EXPECTED mutant outcome and the whole
116:#  reason this control exists.  Sharing one number would also make the SETUP message unable
117:#  to name the phase.  Measured on an idle machine across all 12 lanes: slowest build 1.37 s
118:#  (diskimage_io / diskimage_geom), slowest run 0.31 s (m8820x).  The margins below are ~88x
119:#  and ~190x, deliberately enormous: `gate_ab` uses 3x and that MEASURABLY false-FAILed a
120:#  45-minute battery under subagent load.  A too-tight timeout turns a busy host into a red
121:#  gate, which is the fastest way to get a gate switched off.
122:#  #537, 2026-09-18: a 13th lane, fbpending_bound, links ~221 build/ objects -- build 3.9-4.5 s,
123:#  run 1.9-2.1 s (Opus seat, 8 runs) -- so the margins are now ~27x and ~29x, still an order of
124:#  magnitude above gate_ab's 3x.
125:BUILD_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SELFMUTANT_BUILD_TIMEOUT", "120"))
126:RUN_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SELFMUTANT_RUN_TIMEOUT", "60"))
127:
128:
129:def run_bounded(argv, timeout):
130:    """Run argv, killing the whole PROCESS GROUP on expiry.  Returns (proc_or_None, why)."""
131:    #  *** start_new_session IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART, AND killpg WITHOUT IT WOULD KILL THE
132:    #  GATE. ***  A bare subprocess.run(timeout=) kills only the DIRECT child.  Measured: with
133:    #  a genuine grandchild (the gcc -> cc1 shape) run() raised TimeoutExpired promptly and
134:    #  left TWO orphans spinning; the same shape under start_new_session + killpg left ZERO.
135:    #  The orphan is the real hazard, not a wedge -- an abandoned compiler loading the host is
136:    #  exactly the background load that false-FAILed a battery through a wall-clock oracle.
137:    #
138:    #  (A reviewing seat predicted instead that run()'s post-kill DRAIN would block forever on
139:    #  the grandchild's pipe.  MEASURED FALSE on POSIX: CPython calls process.wait() there,
140:    #  not communicate(), so nothing drains and nothing blocks.  The conclusion survived the
141:    #  refutation of its mechanism, which is why the measurement is recorded and not just the
142:    #  design.)
143:    #
144:    #  Without start_new_session the child shares the GATE's process group -- a command
145:    #  substitution creates no new group in non-interactive bash -- so killpg would be gate
146:    #  suicide.  Measured: the child's pgid was 32 against the gate's 11.
147:    p = subprocess.Popen(argv, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
148:                         text=True, start_new_session=True)
149:    try:
150:        o, e = p.communicate(timeout=timeout)
151:        return p, (o, e)
152:    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
153:        try:
154:            os.killpg(os.getpgid(p.pid), signal.SIGKILL)
155:        except (ProcessLookupError, PermissionError):
156:            pass
157:        try:
158:            p.communicate(timeout=10)
159:        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
160:            pass
161:        return None, "exceeded %gs and was killed" % timeout
162:
163:
164:def build_and_run(tree, harness, binp):
165:    cmd = "cd %s/regress && %s %s -o %s %s -lm" % (tree, CC, EXTRA_FLAGS, binp, harness)
166:    p, res = run_bounded(["bash", "-c", cmd], BUILD_TIMEOUT)
167:    if p is None:
168:        return None, "build phase " + res
169:    if p.returncode != 0:
170:        return None, (res[1].strip().splitlines() or ["?"])[0]
171:    r, res = run_bounded([binp], RUN_TIMEOUT)
172:    if r is None:
173:        return None, "run phase " + res
174:    #  F5: A SIGNAL DEATH IS SETUP, NEVER A VERDICT.  The first version discarded
175:    #  returncode, so a mutant that died by SIGFPE before printing its verdict was scored
176:    #  "the detector did NOT report a failure" -- right colour, wrong diagnosis, and the
177:    #  same class as gate 3's filed #55.  A negative returncode is a signal on POSIX.
178:    if r.returncode < 0:
179:        return None, "killed by signal %d before printing a verdict" % (-r.returncode)
180:    return res[0], None

183:def main(argv):
184:    if len(argv) != 5:

186:        return 2

189:    src_subject = os.path.join(SEC, subject)

194:    #  (3) UNIQUE, not merely present.
195:    n = body.count(old)
196:    if n != 1:
197:        out("SETUP", "anchor occurs %d times in %s (must be exactly 1)" % (n, subject))
198:        print("SELFMUTANT_SETUP")
199:        return 1
200:
201:    #  *** (3b) THE FRAGMENTS MUST ACTUALLY DIFFER.  THIS IS THE SMALLEST APPARATUS FAILURE
202:    #  THERE IS -- `cp x.old x.new`, ZERO characters of difference. ***  Constructed and
203:    #  MEASURED by a seat asked for the smallest escape: the anchor is unique, the write
204:    #  lands, both arms are byte-identical, the detector correctly does not fail, and the
205:    #  helper printed "mutant applied but the detector did NOT report a failure" -- the exact
206:    #  false sentence this round exists to remove, from a two-file copy.
207:    if old == new:
208:        out("SETUP", "the .old and .new fragments are identical -- there is no mutant")
209:        print("SELFMUTANT_SETUP")
210:        return 1
211:
212:    tmp = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="selfmutant_")
213:    try:
214:        #  *** BOTH ARMS BUILD AT THE SAME PATH, AND THAT IS A MEASURED REQUIREMENT, NOT
215:        #  TIDINESS. ***  The old shape built each arm in its own directory.  Harmless today,
216:        #  but it makes the binary-identity check below SILENTLY VACUOUS the moment anyone
217:        #  adds `-g`: MEASURED, with -g and -g3 the PRISTINE arm's binary DIFFERS FROM ITSELF
218:        #  across two tree paths (debug info records the compilation directory), so "the two
219:        #  arms differ" becomes true unconditionally and the check stops catching anything
220:        #  without ever going red.  At one path, with -g3, pristine-vs-pristine is IDENTICAL
221:        #  and mutant-vs-pristine still differs.  It also saves an entire link_tree.
222:        tree = os.path.join(tmp, "arm")
223:        os.makedirs(tree)
224:        #  real_file goes to BOTH mirrors.  It used to reach only the src one, so a subject
225:        #  anywhere else -- regress/, tools/ -- was symlinked pristine into both arms and the
226:        #  mutant text landed NOWHERE.  Each call ignores a real_file that is not under it,
227:        #  so passing both is safe and makes an out-of-src subject WORK rather than merely be
228:        #  refused.  Nothing uses one today; refusing would not have been simpler.
229:        link_tree(os.path.join(SEC, "regress"), os.path.join(tree, "regress"),
230:                  real_file=src_subject, real_text=body)
231:        link_tree(os.path.join(SEC, "src"), os.path.join(tree, "src"),
232:                  real_file=src_subject, real_text=body)
233:
234:        tree_subject = os.path.join(tree, subject)
235:        binp = os.path.join(tree, "d")
236:        digest = {}
237:
238:        for arm, text in (("pristine", body), ("mutant", body.replace(old, new, 1))):
239:            #  Rewrite the ONE real file in place, then prove what is there.
240:            if os.path.islink(tree_subject):
241:                out("SETUP", "%s is a SYMLINK in the tree -- the mutant would not land"
242:                    % subject)
243:                print("SELFMUTANT_SETUP")
244:                return 1
245:            io_write(tree_subject, text)
246:
247:            #  *** (3c) THE MUTATION MUST HAVE LANDED, AND THE TEST IS EXACT TEXT, NOT A
248:            #  FRAGMENT COUNT. ***  The obvious form -- "the file contains `new` exactly
249:            #  once" -- is wrong in BOTH directions and was caught before it shipped.  It is
250:            #  blind to the escapes below, AND it FALSE-ALARMS on a currently-green lane:
251:            #  MEASURED, diskimage_io's `new` is a suffix-substring of its `old` and that text
252:            #  occurs FIVE times in src/disk/diskimage.c, so the lane would have gone SETUP
253:            #  the day the check landed.  Three seats found this independently.
254:            #
255:            #  The helper already computed exactly what each arm should contain, so compare
256:            #  against that.  Immune to substring games and recurrence; it also subsumes
257:            #  "is the pristine arm really pristine" for free, and catches a truncated write,
258:            #  newline mangling, or a write misdirected by case-folding.  Read back with
259:            #  newline="" so universal-newline translation cannot mask a corrupted write.
260:            got = io.open(tree_subject, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
261:            if got != text:

1:/*
2: *  #442 fbpending: an OFFLINE DIRECT-CALL DETECTOR for the pending-interrupt bound.
3: *
4: *  *** THIS IS NOT A REPRODUCTION AND MUST NEVER BE RECORDED AS ONE. ***  Under this
5: *  project's witness ladder it is rung 1: it #includes the device's own translation
6: *  unit and calls timer_tickN() directly, with no machine description, no address
7: *  decode and no device dispatch.  It still compiles and still fails with all of that
8: *  removed, which is the mechanical discriminator the ladder gives.  The reproduction
9: *  is the rung-3 cold-debugger witness; this is the row that DEFENDS the fix.
10: *
11: *  WHY IT IS NEEDED IN ADDITION TO THE RUNG-3 ROW, measured rather than assumed.  The
12: *  rung-3 drain probe measures a backlog by letting DEVICE_TICK deliver ticks the guest
13: *  can see.  Two consequences, both measured against real mutants:
14: *
15: *    - a bound placed in DEVICE_TICK instead of in the callback (mutant m2) is applied
16: *      by the very mechanism the probe uses to observe, so the probe reads a bounded
17: *      counter and passes.  A direct call never runs DEVICE_TICK at all.
18: *    - a bound with no ABSOLUTE ceiling (mutant m6, cap == (int)freq alone) is correct
19: *      at cats' 50 MHz and overflows at a higher emulated_hz.  A guest probe cannot
20: *      reach that in bounded time: the core delivers at most TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP *
21: *      TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY == 68,157,440 increments per wall second, so 2^31 of them
22: *      take 31.5 s AT BEST.  A direct call does 2^31 increments in seconds.
23: *
24: *  It compiles the SHIPPED source, not a copy of it -- a detector that restates the
25: *  code it is testing is the vacuity taxonomy's first entry.
26: */
27:

37:    symbols that dropping main.o leaves undefined.  */

52:    sees WHICH number moved when a row is added or removed.

56:    a human derives is a count that drifts, and here it was wrong before it ever shipped.  */

60:static int rows;
61:
62:static void row(const char *name, int good, const char *fmt, ...)
63:{
64:	va_list ap;
65:	printf("  %-4s %-44s ", good ? "ok" : "FAIL", name);
66:	va_start(ap, fmt);
67:	vprintf(fmt, ap);
68:	va_end(ap);
69:	printf("\n");
70:	rows ++;
71:	if (!good)
72:		failures ++;
73:}
74:
75:
76:/*
77: *  Drive one timer through `n` producer ticks and report the high-water mark.
78: *
79: *  The counter is read back after EVERY call rather than only at the end, because the
80: *  interesting failure is transient: a bound that resets to 1 makes the final value
81: *  small even when the peak was INT_MIN.  A final-value-only check would be green on
82: *  the very defect this file exists for.
83: */
84:static void drive(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, long long n,
85:	int *peak, int *trough)
86:{
87:	long long i;
88:	*peak = INT_MIN;
89:	*trough = INT_MAX;
90:	for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
91:		switch (nr) {
92:		case 0: timer_tick0(NULL, d); break;
93:		case 1: timer_tick1(NULL, d); break;
94:		case 2: timer_tick2(NULL, d); break;
95:		case 3: timer_tick3(NULL, d); break;
96:		}
97:		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] > *peak)
98:			*peak = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
99:		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] < *trough)
100:			*trough = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
101:	}
102:}
103:
104:
105:/*  Arm timer `nr` exactly as a guest would: a control write then a load write,
106:    through the device's own register handler, so the model's own interpretation of
107:    the two registers is what is under test rather than a re-derivation of it.  */
108:static void arm(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, uint32_t load, uint32_t control)
109:{
110:	d->timer_control[nr] = control;
111:	d->timer_load[nr] = load & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
112:	reload_timer_value(&fake_cpu, d, nr);
113:}

116:int main(int argc, char *argv[])
117:{
118:	struct footbridge_data *d;
119:	int peak, trough, nr;
120:	long long deep = 0;
121:	double f;

123:	if (argc > 1)
124:		deep = atoll(argv[1]);
125:
126:	fake_cpu.machine = &fake_machine;
127:	timer_init();

129:	printf("==============================================================\n");
130:	printf("fbpending OFFLINE BOUND DETECTOR (rung 1 -- a DETECTOR, not a\n");
131:	printf("reproduction).  TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP=%d  TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY=%.0f\n",
132:	    TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP, (double)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
133:	printf("  the most one timer can be ticked in one wall second: %.0f\n",
134:	    (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
135:	printf("==============================================================\n");

137:	/*

139:	 *  cannot drive the counter at all, every bound row passes vacuously.  Drive an
140:	 *  ARMED timer 4096 times with the bound deliberately out of reach and require
141:	 *  the counter to actually move.
142:	 */
143:	d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
144:	fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
145:	arm(d, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);
146:	drive(d, 0, 4096, &peak, &trough);
147:	row("R0 the harness can drive the counter at all", peak >= 4096,
148:	    "peak after 4096 direct ticks = %d (expect >= 4096 with no bound)", peak);
149:	free(d);

151:	/*
152:	 *  R1 -- THE PROPERTY.  For each of the four timers, and for a spread of
153:	 *  guest-reachable (emulated_hz, load, control) triples, the counter must stay
154:	 *  inside [0, ceiling] across more increments than the ceiling itself.
155:	 *
156:	 *  emulated_hz is included in the sweep because it is USER-REACHABLE: main.c:376
157:	 *  sets machine->emulated_hz from -I, and emul_parse.c:621 from a config file.  A
158:	 *  cap of the form (int)freq with no absolute ceiling is correct at 50 MHz and
159:	 *  becomes INT_MAX at the top of that range -- which is mutant m6, and which no
160:	 *  row run only at cats' default would ever see.
161:	 */
162:	{
163:		static const struct { int hz; uint32_t load; uint32_t ctrl;
164:			const char *what; } cases[] = {
165:		    {    50000000, 0x000100, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, load 256/256" },
166:		    {    50000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "cats, load 1  (50 MHz)" },
167:		    {    63750000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "netwinder, load 1" },
168:		    {  1000000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I 1e9, load 1" },
169:		    {  2147483647, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 1" },
170:		    {  2147483647, 0x000000, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 0 (2^24)" },
171:		    {    50000000, 0xFFFFFF, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, slowest legal" },
172:		};
173:		const int ceiling = (int)(TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * (int)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
174:		size_t c;
175:		for (c = 0; c < sizeof(cases)/sizeof(cases[0]); c++) {
176:			int worst_peak = INT_MIN, worst_trough = INT_MAX;
177:			d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
178:			fake_machine.emulated_hz = cases[c].hz;
179:			for (nr = 0; nr < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; nr++) {
180:				arm(d, nr, cases[c].load, cases[c].ctrl);
181:				drive(d, nr, (long long)ceiling + 4096, &peak, &trough);
182:				if (peak > worst_peak)
183:					worst_peak = peak;
184:				if (trough < worst_trough)
185:					worst_trough = trough;
186:			}
187:			f = (double)cases[c].hz /
188:			    (double)footbridge_effective_cycles(cases[c].load & TIMER_MAX_VAL,
189:			    cases[c].ctrl, 1);
190:			row("R1 bounded across all 4 timers",
191:			    worst_peak <= ceiling && worst_trough >= 0,
192:			    "%-28s rate %12.2f Hz  peak %11d trough %11d  (ceiling %d)",
193:			    cases[c].what, f, worst_peak, worst_trough, ceiling);
194:
195:			/*
196:			 *  R5 -- THE BOUND IS THE TIMER'S OWN RATE, AS AN EQUALITY.
197:			 *
198:			 *  *** R1 ABOVE IS AN INEQUALITY AGAINST A CONSTANT, AND A
199:			 *  PASS-2 SEAT MEASURED FIVE MUTANTS WALKING THROUGH IT. ***  It
200:			 *  computes `f` -- the case's own delivered rate -- PRINTS it,
201:			 *  and then compares nothing to it, so ANY bound between R0's
202:			 *  floor and the ceiling satisfies it.
203:			 *
204:			 *  The one that matters is `double bound = freq / 100.0;` -- ONE
205:			 *  TOKEN.  Measured at rung 3 with a 0.5 s accrual: the shipped
206:			 *  build owes the guest 375 interrupts and that mutant delivers
207:			 *  FOUR.  It destroys 99% of the guest's clock and BOTH detectors
208:			 *  said PASS.  That is exactly the failure timer.h's "THE BACKLOG
209:			 *  IS RETAINED" paragraph exists to prevent.
210:			 *
211:			 *  An equality closes it, needs no new oracle, and costs nothing:
212:			 *  `f` was already computed for the message above.
213:			 */
214:			{
215:				int want = (int) f;
216:				if (want < 1)
217:					want = 1;
218:				if (want > ceiling)
219:					want = ceiling;
220:				row("R5 the bound IS this timer's rate",
221:				    worst_peak == want,
222:				    "%-28s peak %11d  want %11d", cases[c].what,
223:				    worst_peak, want);
224:			}
225:			/*
226:			 *  R3 -- ONE INTERRUPT IS STILL OWED, and it is a separate row
227:			 *  from R1 because a bound that resets to ZERO satisfies R1
228:			 *  perfectly while dropping an interrupt the guest was owed.
229:			 *  luna88k resets to 1 for exactly this reason

236:			row("R3 the reset still owes one interrupt",

239:			    cases[c].what, worst_trough);
240:			free(d);
241:		}
242:	}

244:	/*

247:	 *  the counter must never be negative.  Skipped unless a depth is given, because
248:	 *  it costs a few seconds -- but the default depth is still above any cap the

262:	}

264:	/*

266:	 *
267:	 *  *** THE CASE LOOP ABOVE ARMS ALL FOUR TIMERS WITH IDENTICAL PARAMETERS AND
268:	 *  ARMS EACH EXACTLY ONCE, so it is structurally blind to two mutants a pass-2
269:	 *  seat measured walking through everything: *** a bound FROZEN after the first
270:	 *  arm (recomputation stops mattering) and a bound written to `[0]` instead of
271:	 *  `[timer_nr]` (every timer shares timer 0's).  Neither can be seen by a table
272:	 *  where all four timers always agree and nothing is ever re-armed.
273:	 *
274:	 *  So: arm two timers at DIFFERENT rates, then RE-ARM one at a third, and
275:	 *  require each timer's bound to track its own current rate.  Same oracle as
276:	 *  R5 -- the peak the counter reaches -- so no new oracle is introduced.
277:	 */
278:	{

284:		arm(r6, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);			/*  50 MHz  */
285:		arm(r6, 1, 256, TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);	/*  762 Hz  */
286:
287:		/*  RE-ARM timer 0 much slower.  A frozen bound keeps 50e6 here.  */
288:		arm(r6, 0, 256, TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);
289:		drive(r6, 0, 4096, &peak, &trough);
290:		row("R6 a re-armed timer's bound follows its NEW rate",
291:		    peak == 762, "timer 0 re-armed 50 MHz -> 762 Hz: peak %d want 762",
292:		    peak);
293:
294:		/*  And timer 1 must carry ITS own bound, not timer 0's.  */
295:		drive(r6, 1, 4096, &peak, &trough);
296:		row("R6b each timer carries its OWN bound",
297:		    peak == 762, "timer 1: peak %d want 762", peak);
298:		free(r6);
299:	}
300:
301:	/*
302:	 *  @@SELFCHECK@@ -- the SECOND failability control, asking a different question
303:	 *  from the self-mutant in gate_offline.sh.  That one breaks dev_footbridge.c and
304:	 *  demands a NAMED row notice, so it catches a fixture that has stopped reaching
305:	 *  the code.  This one breaks nothing and feeds row() itself a deliberate
306:	 *  mismatch, so it catches a comparator edited into always-passing -- a state in
307:	 *  which every row prints `ok` and the row count AND the verdict token both
308:	 *  survive.  Neither catches the other's failure.
309:	 *
310:	 *  The token in the row name is load-bearing: selfmutant.py filters exactly it,
311:	 *  and without it these deliberate FAIL lines would be read as a mutant kill for
312:	 *  a mutant nothing caught.
313:	 */
314:	{
315:		int sc_f = failures, sc_r = rows, sc_bad = 0;
316:
317:		row("@@SELFCHECK@@/false", 0, "a deliberate mismatch");
318:		if (failures <= sc_f)
319:			sc_bad++;
320:		failures = sc_f; rows = sc_r;
321:
322:		row("@@SELFCHECK@@/true", 1, "a deliberate match");
323:		if (failures != sc_f)
324:			sc_bad++;
325:		failures = sc_f; rows = sc_r;
326:
327:		/*  sc_bad is a SEPARATE local that survives the restore above -- restoring
328:		    `failures` is what makes the sentinel non-destructive, so the evidence
329:		    cannot live in `failures` itself.  */
330:		row("SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail", sc_bad == 0,
331:		    "both directions");
332:	}
333:
334:	/*
335:	 *  R4 -- THE AGGREGATE, and it exists for a mechanical reason rather than a
336:	 *  semantic one: selfmutant.py requires the pinned row id to name EXACTLY ONE
337:	 *  row, and R1/R3 each print seven times (once per emulated_hz/load case).  A pin
338:	 *  of "R1 " would match seven lines and the helper refuses it.  R4 is the unique
339:	 *  name the lane pins, and any case failing above takes it red.
340:	 */
341:	row("R4 every case bounded (the selfmutant pin)", failures == 0,
342:	    "%d row failure(s) above", failures);
343:
344:	/*
345:	 *  IDENTITY -- the row count asserts itself.  Without it, DELETING a row is
346:	 *  invisible: every remaining row prints ok, the verdict token survives, and the
347:	 *  gate's `rows actually run` floor only notices a drop BELOW the floor.  This is
348:	 *  the constblind shape, and gate section E hard-fails a new differential lacking
349:	 *  it.
350:	 *
351:	 *  `rows + 1` counts this row itself, which has not been printed yet -- arguments
352:	 *  are evaluated before the call.  The expectation is CONDITIONAL on `deep`,
353:	 *  because R2 only runs when a depth is given, and hardcoding one value would make
354:	 *  a no-depth run red for no defect.  The SELFCHECK block restores `rows`, so its
355:	 *  two deliberate rows do not count while its verdict row does.
356:	 */
357:	row("IDENTITY: row count", rows + 1 == (deep > 0 ? IDENT_DEEP : IDENT_SHALLOW),
358:	    "%d rows (expect %d with R2, %d without)",
359:	    rows + 1, IDENT_DEEP, IDENT_SHALLOW);
360:
361:	/*  #537: the house verdict token, `<WORD>_PASS' / `<WORD>_FAIL'.  This printed
362:	    `FBBOUND_VERDICT=PASS', which selfmutant.py's `\b([A-Z0-9_]+_PASS)\b' can never
363:	    match -- the one deviation that kept this harness's self-mutant lane out of gate 2
364:	    since #442 (diagnosed as `fbsmlane', 2026-08-27).  gate_offline.sh greps this line.  */
365:	printf("\nFBBOUND_%s\n", failures ? "FAIL" : "PASS");
366:	return failures ? 1 : 0;
367:}

		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;

WHY THIS MUTANT, AND WHY THIS ROW.

The anchor is #442's own reset, `d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;` in
footbridge_pending() at src/devices/dev_footbridge.c.  Changing the 1 to a 0 leaves the file
compiling, leaves the BOUND intact, and leaves the overflow fixed -- it breaks only the
"one interrupt is still owed" half, coalescing the debt to nothing instead of to one tick.

WHY THAT MUTANT AND NOT A BIGGER ONE.  Deleting the bound entirely is caught by seven R1
rows and is the pre-fix state, so it tests nothing the pre-fix arm does not.  This one is the
narrowest edit that a reader would plausibly make -- "reset to zero" reads tidier than "reset
to one" -- and it is invisible to every row except R3.

MEASURED, 2026-08-20, all seven emulated_hz/load cases:
  pristine            R3 lowest value the counter ever held: 1     FBBOUND_VERDICT=PASS
  1 -> 0              R3 lowest value the counter ever held: 0     FBBOUND_VERDICT=FAIL, 7 rows
  and R1 stays GREEN on all seven cases in the mutant arm, which is the point: the bound is
  still doing its job, so a verdict-only or R1-only assertion would score this as fine.

*** THE PIN IS "R4 every case bounded", NOT R3, AND THAT IS MECHANICAL. ***  selfmutant.py
requires the pinned row id to name EXACTLY ONE row (it refuses ambiguity, having been bitten
by a rowid as ordinary as "fail" matching several).  R1 and R3 each print SEVEN times, once
per case, so neither can be pinned.  R4 is an aggregate row added for exactly this purpose
and it goes red whenever any case above does.

The rung-3 reproduction for this defect is regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py and it is a
DIFFERENT artifact with a different job: it drives real guest MMIO on an unmodified -E cats.
This harness calls timer_tickN() directly, which the witness ladder classes as never a
reproduction.  Both ship because neither alone catches what the other does -- measured: a
mutant deleting the absolute ceiling PASSES the rung-3 probe on every timer and is caught
only here, at an emulated_hz above 68 MHz.

#537, 2026-09-18: the harness now prints the house verdict token, FBBOUND_PASS / FBBOUND_FAIL.  The
table above is the 2026-08-20 output as it printed THEN (FBBOUND_VERDICT=...), kept verbatim.

 succeeded in 161ms:
745:#  INVALIDATE_ALL -> INVALIDATE_VADDR as a real emulator and BOOTED THE luna88k RIG
746:#  WITH IT: 1:1:1 to `login:`, no diagnostics.  The purge is gutted to virtual page
747:#  0 -- under-invalidation, the dangerous direction -- while the CALL COUNT stays
748:#  bit-identical.  A count-based offline row cannot catch it, and THAT THREE-RUN
749:#  BOOT-TO-LOGIN TRIAL did not; a different workload or assertion on the same rig
750:#  might.  The likely masking mechanism is that the guest issues ~603,000 SCR
751:#  flushes per boot, each doing its own INVALIDATE_ALL -- BELIEVED, since a
752:#  successful boot alongside a plausible mechanism is not established attribution.
753:#  ARGUMENT-INSPECTING ROWS are what catch it, F1 AND F2 both, which is why the

755:#  totals.

891:selfmutant_one() {   # harness subject stem row-id [extra-cc-flags]
892:    local h="$1" subj="$2" stem="$3" row="$4" xflags="${5:-}"
893:    local o="$HERE/selfmutants/$stem.old" n="$HERE/selfmutants/$stem.new"
894:    if [ ! -f "$o" ] || [ ! -f "$n" ]; then
895:        check "selfmutant $stem: fragments present" "no" "yes"; return
896:    fi
897:    local out
898:    #  The extra flags are the caller's, because the caller owns the real compile line.
899:    #  memory_rw needs -fno-optimize-sibling-calls, which THIS FILE calls load-bearing for
900:    #  the loop-not-recursion row; the helper hardcoded one flag set and so compiled that
901:    #  harness differently from the battery, which the pristine control arm cannot detect
902:    #  because both arms are then consistently wrong.
903:    out=$(SELFMUTANT_EXTRA_FLAGS="$xflags"           python3 "$HERE/selfmutant.py" "$h" "$subj" "$o" "$n" "$row" 2>&1)
904:    printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -E '^       ' | sed 's/^/    /'
905:    #  ASSERT THE OK TOKEN IS PRESENT, never "FAIL is absent": a setup error emits
906:    #  neither, so a negative form is satisfied by a control that never ran.
907:    check "selfmutant $stem: the detector still detects, via its named row" \
908:          "$(printf '%s' "$out" | grep -c 'SELFMUTANT_OK')" "1"
909:}

912:#  block from all five detectors and measured: gate 2 stayed PASS at the SAME check count with
913:#  ZERO red rows, byte-identical to baseline, and all five self-mutants still OK.
914:#
915:#  It is unprotected PRECISELY BECAUSE THE BOOKKEEPING IS CORRECT: the row restores `rows`, so
916:#  it is invisible to the only mechanism this gate has for noticing a deleted row -- the

918:#  that is the SOLE detector of something, so deleting one is a red row rather than a quieter

2822:def _st_apply(path, old, new, want=1):
2823:    src = read(path)
2824:    n = src.count(old)
2825:    if n != want:
2826:        raise RuntimeError("MUTANT DID NOT APPLY: %r x%d in %s (want %d)"
2827:                           % (old[:56], n, os.path.basename(path), want))
2828:    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as fh:
2829:        fh.write(src.replace(old, new))

2833:    with open(path, "a", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as fh:
2834:        fh.write(text)

2837:def selftest(verbose=False):
2838:    import shutil
2839:    import subprocess
2840:    import tempfile
2841:
2842:    src_regress = DEFAULT_REGRESS
2843:    me = os.path.abspath(__file__)
2844:    root = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="probewiring_selftest_")
2845:    results = []
2846:
2847:    #  *** THE BASELINE MUST BE GREEN BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE FIRST VERSION WAS NOT. ***
2848:    #  fresh() copied EVERY .py from the live tree, so when a concurrent round dropped an
2849:    #  unclassified probe into regress/, M0/M2b/M5a all went red and the selftest announced
2850:    #  "THE CHECKER CAN NO LONGER BE SHOWN TO FAIL".  Right colour, WRONG DIAGNOSIS: it
2851:    #  blamed the checker for an APPARATUS state -- gate_offline's F5 shape, and the
2852:    #  dangerous one, because it points the reader at the wrong file.
2853:    #
2854:    #  So the baseline copies exactly the MANIFEST set.  Live-tree content can no longer
2855:    #  perturb a mutant, and M6 still tests the unclassified-arrival path by ADDING one.
2856:    #  A MANIFEST entry with no source file is a SETUP FAULT here, never a mutant result --
2857:    #  absorb_census.py's rule that a crash is a fault and never a detection.
2858:    def fresh():
2859:        work = tempfile.mkdtemp(dir=root)
2860:        rg = os.path.join(work, "regress")
2861:        os.makedirs(rg)
2862:        #  #471: THE COPY MUST MIRROR THE TREE, NOT FLATTEN IT.  This loop was
2863:        #  `os.listdir`, so once the checker could see nested detectors the selftest
2864:        #  copy still could not -- and every nested MANIFEST entry became a GHOST in
2865:        #  the copy, reddening the unmutated M0 control.
2866:        #
2867:        #  That is the SAME blind spot one level up, inside the harness that tests
2868:        #  for it: a flat copy cannot exercise a recursive scan.
2869:        for f in os.listdir(src_regress):
2870:            if f.endswith(".sh"):
2871:                shutil.copyfile(os.path.join(src_regress, f), os.path.join(rg, f))
2872:        for rel in MANIFEST:
2873:            if not rel.endswith(".py"):
2874:                continue
2875:            src = os.path.join(src_regress, rel.replace("/", os.sep))
2876:            if not os.path.isfile(src):
2877:                continue
2878:            dst = os.path.join(rg, rel.replace("/", os.sep))
2879:            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst), exist_ok=True)
2880:            shutil.copyfile(src, dst)
2881:        chk = os.path.join(work, "check_probe_wiring.py")
2882:        shutil.copyfile(me, chk)
2883:        return rg, chk
2884:
2885:    absent = [f for f in sorted(MANIFEST) if not os.path.isfile(os.path.join(src_regress, f))]
2886:    if absent:
2887:        print("PROBEWIRING_SELFTEST_SETUP  the MANIFEST names files regress/ does not have:")
2888:        for f in absent:
2889:            print("    %s" % f)
2890:        print("  fix the manifest first; a selftest on a broken baseline proves nothing.")
2891:        return 2
2892:
2893:    def case(name, want_rc, want_text, chk, rg, extra=(), forbid=None):
2894:        p = subprocess.run([sys.executable, chk, "--regress", rg] + list(extra),
2895:                           capture_output=True, text=True)
2896:        out = p.stdout + p.stderr
2897:        ok = (p.returncode == want_rc) and (want_text in out)
2898:        if forbid is not None and forbid in out:
2899:            ok = False
2900:        results.append(ok)
2901:        print("  %s  %-48s rc=%d want=%d" % ("ok  " if ok else "FAIL", name,
2902:                                             p.returncode, want_rc))
2903:        if verbose or not ok:
2904:            for l in out.splitlines():
2905:                if want_text in l or l.startswith("PROBEWIRING"):
2906:                    print("          %s" % l.strip()[:160])
2907:        if not ok:
2908:            print("      --- full output ---\n%s" % out)
2909:
2910:    print("=" * 96)
2911:    print("  check_probe_wiring --selftest   (mutants on copies; the repo is not touched)")
2912:    print("=" * 96)
2913:
2914:    rg, chk = fresh()
2915:    case("M0  unmutated copy is GREEN", 0, "PROBEWIRING_PASS", chk, rg)

2918:    #  broke this selftest the day an exemption was discharged.
2919:    EX = _pick_exempt_detector()
2920:    if EX is None:

2969:         "GATED WITNESS  sh4pcic_witness.py", chk, rg)

2973:    #  the day that block was re-dated, M5b would have run on a tree with nothing expired, gone
2974:    #  GREEN, and failed the selftest.  The frozen-claim-about-a-table-designed-to-change that

3153:            today = argv[i + 1]

1:#!/bin/bash
2:# GATE 1 -- both trees rebuild clean from committed source.
3:#
4:# There is NO VPATH in this project. `build/` is an in-place copy compiled from est/, and
5:# /tmp/gxsec-build is an in-place copy compiled from GXEMUL-SEC/. An edit that is not
6:# propagated into the compile tree silently tests the OLD binary, so this gate re-syncs
7:# both from their authoritative tree before building.
8:#
9:# Two failure modes this gate is shaped to catch, both of which have happened:
10:#
11:#  * cpus/*.o must be deleted explicitly. cpu_mips_instr.c is #included into cpu_mips.o
12:#    rather than compiled separately, so make does not see it as a dependency and a stale
13:#    object survives an ordinary rebuild.
14:#  * /tmp/gxsec-build's Makefile is GENERATED from Makefile.skel and is not tracked. A
15:#    tree recreated with `cp -a` has none, and make then exits rc=2 having built nothing
16:#    -- which looks like "0 errors" if you only grep for the word error. The object COUNT
17:#    is the real check, which is why it is asserted rather than reported.
18:set -u
19:HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
20:. "$HERE/lib.sh"
21:
22:EST=$ROOT/est
23:SEC=$ROOT/GXEMUL-SEC
24:PMAX_TREE=$ROOT/build
25:ARC_TREE=/tmp/gxsec-build
26:RIG=$ROOT/gxemul_arc_rig
27:
28:gate_begin "clean-build"
29:
30:# #396: UNPUBLISH FIRST. The published arc binary is withdrawn here, at the very top,
31:# and republished only after gate_end returns 0. #395 removed it inside the publish
32:# block instead, which meant every route that never REACHED that block left the stale
33:# copies in place: `need_file` below exits 77 (SKIP) before it, and so does any signal,
34:# `set -u` abort, or death inside make. Measured by a seat replaying the gate: the SKIP
35:# route left both stale copies published, and run.sh:94-99 then calls the battery
36:# REGRESS_PASS_WITH_GAPS. That is not a hypothetical -- $ARC_TREE is /tmp/gxsec-build,
37:# in WSL's /tmp, which is cleared on reboot, while $RIG/gxsec-gxemul survives; so the
38:# first gate 1 after a reboot takes exactly this path.
39:#
40:# #398 CORRECTS TWO THINGS #396 GOT WRONG HERE.
41:#
42:# FIRST, THE CLAIM. #396 stated that withdraw-then-republish makes "a published binary
43:# exists" mean "the last gate 1 that ran, PASSED", for every termination route. *** THAT
44:# IS FALSE, and a seat measured it. *** Publication sat BETWEEN the verdict and the
45:# stale-verdict guard, so when that guard fired the binary was already published: exit 1
46:# with both copies present and fresh. #398 moves the guard ABOVE publication, which makes
47:# the invariant hold for the routes that reach the bottom -- but it STILL does not hold
48:# in general, because a check appended below the publish block is not seen by a verdict
49:# taken above it. The honest statement is the narrow one: NOTHING IS PUBLISHED UNLESS
50:# gate_end RETURNED 0. The general property needs publication to move OUT of this script
51:# into run.sh, conditioned on gate 1's exit status -- a gate cannot verify its own
52:# publication after its own verdict. That is filed, not attempted here.
53:#
54:# SECOND, THE METHOD. #396 DELETED the published copies, and that introduced a denial of
55:# service: a SKIP destroys both, and NOTHING IN THIS REPO EVER CREATES /tmp/gxsec-build
56:# (measured with `grep -rn gxsec-build`), so a gate 1 run after a reboot -- when WSL's
57:# /tmp is empty -- wipes the rig's only binary and cannot replace it. Withdrawing by
58:# RENAME keeps the honesty (no published name during or after a failed run) without the
59:# destruction: the artifact stays recoverable at *.withdrawn.
60:withdraw() {                     # $1 = published path
61:    [ -e "$1" ] || return 0
62:    mv -f "$1" "$1.withdrawn" 2>/dev/null || rm -f "$1"
63:}
64:withdraw /tmp/gxsec-gxemul
65:if [ -d "$RIG" ]; then withdraw "$RIG/gxsec-gxemul"; fi
66:
67:need_file "$EST/src" "$SEC/src" "$PMAX_TREE" "$ARC_TREE"
68:
69:# list_diffs <dirA> <dirB> -- relative paths of source files that differ, or exist on
70:# only one side.
71:#
72:# Deliberately NOT `diff -rq --include='*.c'`. diff has --exclude but NO --include (that
73:# is a grep option), so that command exits 2 with "unrecognized option" and prints
74:# nothing -- and with stderr discarded it looks exactly like "no differences". Both the
75:# divergence check and the sync check below were written that way, so both were comparing
170:list_diffs "$EST/src" "$SEC/src" | sort > "$LOGDIR/divergent_actual.txt"
171:echo "$DIVERGENT" | awk '{print $1}' | sort > "$LOGDIR/divergent_expected.txt"
172:comm -23 "$LOGDIR/divergent_actual.txt" "$LOGDIR/divergent_expected.txt" \
173:    > "$LOGDIR/divergent_unexpected.txt"
174:n_actual=$(grep -c '^' < "$LOGDIR/divergent_actual.txt")
175:n_unexpected=$(grep -c '^' < "$LOGDIR/divergent_unexpected.txt")
176:if [ "$n_unexpected" != 0 ]; then
177:    note "UNEXPECTED divergence (likely an un-propagated correction):"
178:    sed 's/^/         /' "$LOGDIR/divergent_unexpected.txt"
179:fi
180:note "divergent files seen ($n_actual): $(tr '\n' ' ' < "$LOGDIR/divergent_actual.txt")"
181:
182:# #396: THE OTHER DIRECTION. Only `comm -23` was computed -- actual-minus-expected -- so
183:# an entry that is allowlisted but NO LONGER DIVERGENT could never fail. Measured by a
184:# seat replaying this gate: normalise disk/diskimage.c (which is literally what its own
185:# reason instructs) and keep the entry, and the gate still reports PASS (14 checks). A
186:# silently-stale exemption is precisely the defect #394 claimed to be fixing, so #394
187:# shipped without the assertion that would have proved its own point.
188:comm -13 "$LOGDIR/divergent_actual.txt" "$LOGDIR/divergent_expected.txt" \
189:    > "$LOGDIR/divergent_stale.txt"
190:n_stale=$(grep -c '^' < "$LOGDIR/divergent_stale.txt")
191:if [ "$n_stale" != 0 ]; then
192:    note "STALE allowlist entries (listed as divergent, but the files now agree):"
193:    sed 's/^/         /' "$LOGDIR/divergent_stale.txt"
194:fi
195:
196:# #396: AND THE ENTRIES MUST BE REAL PATHS. The parser takes the first whitespace field
197:# of each line, which silently turns any stray line into an allowlist entry. The
198:# dangerous instance is not exotic: a reason WRAPPED onto a second line donates that
199:# line's first word. Measured -- a genuine divergence went unexpected=1 (RED) to 0
200:# (GREEN) that way, and the reasons here run 100-300 characters in a file wrapped at
201:# about 90 columns, so wrapping one is an ordinary edit, not an act of sabotage.
202:# Requiring every parsed entry to exist in at least one tree makes prose fail loudly:
203:# "Normalise", "The" and "every" are not files. This also catches a blank line, a
204:# whitespace-only line, and a `# comment` line, none of which the comparison notices.
205:n_bogus=0
206:while IFS= read -r _e; do
207:    [ -n "$_e" ] || { n_bogus=$((n_bogus+1)); continue; }
208:    # #398: -f, not -e. `-e` is true for DIRECTORIES, so a reason wrapping onto a line
209:    # that begins `devices ...` left this row GREEN -- vacuous for precisely the input
210:    # the comment above claimed it caught. Measured by a seat.
211:    if [ ! -f "$EST/src/$_e" ] && [ ! -f "$SEC/src/$_e" ]; then
212:        note "ALLOWLIST ENTRY IS NOT A FILE IN EITHER TREE: '$_e'"
213:        n_bogus=$((n_bogus+1))
214:    fi
215:done < "$LOGDIR/divergent_expected.txt"
216:
217:# A FLOOR as well as a ceiling. The divergence set is known to be non-empty, so an empty
218:# list means the comparison itself broke -- which is exactly how the previous version of
219:# this check passed while measuring nothing.
220:check_min "divergence comparison actually ran" "$n_actual" 1

2418:def _refuse_duplicate_keys():
2419:    with open(os.path.abspath(__file__), "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
2420:        src = fh.read()
2421:    #  *** AN ANCHOR THAT NO LONGER MATCHES IS ITSELF A SETUP FAULT, NOT A REASON TO SKIP,
2422:    #  AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS GUARD DID `continue`. ***  A flagship review MEASURED
2423:    #  the consequence: `MANIFEST =  {` -- one extra space, valid Python, invisible to a
2424:    #  reader -- makes a literal find() miss, the guard skips, and a planted duplicate key
2425:    #  runs GREEN.  That is this file's own thesis turned on itself: a fault in a guard does
2426:    #  not fail loudly, it stops guarding.  The regex tolerates whitespace, and a miss is
2427:    #  now reported rather than shrugged at.
2428:    for name in ("MANIFEST", "EXEMPT"):
2429:        m = re.search(r"^%s\s*=\s*\{" % re.escape(name), src, re.M)
2430:        if m is None:
2431:            print("PROBEWIRING_SETUP_FAULT  the %s literal could not be located in this"
2432:                  " file's own source." % name)
2433:            print("  The duplicate-key guard cannot run, and a duplicate would pass"
2434:                  " UNSEEN.  Fix the anchor, do not delete the check.")
2435:            sys.exit(2)
2436:        i = m.start()
2437:        j = src.find("\n}", i)
2438:        seen, dupes = set(), []
2439:        for k in re.findall(r'^\s*"([^"]+)"\s*:', src[i:j], re.M):

The helper matches its pin against the whole printed row, including the case description. That appears to allow a unique R3 pin without adding a row or changing IDENTITY counts. I also found that `run.sh` continues after gate 1 fails or skips, so buildless gate 2 is not unreachable in the battery. Keeping it red can still be justified, but that premise needs correcting.

1140:#
1141:#      ieee_store            no date at all   -> never expires
1142:#      ieee_store:2026-8-1   unpadded, 19 days past -> never expires
1143:#      ieee_store:garbage    not a date       -> never expires
1144:#
1145:#  MECHANISM.  `${e##*:}` strips to the LAST colon -- and with NO colon it strips nothing and
1146:#  returns the whole entry, so `ieee_store` was compared as a date: 's' > '2', false, unexpired.
1147:#  The `""` arm only catches `stem:` with an empty tail, which is the one malformed case that
1148:#  was handled.  For the unpadded form the compare is worse than useless: `2026-8-1` against
1149:#  `2026-08-20` matches through `2026-`, then '8' > '0', so a date 19 days PAST sorts as
1150:#  FUTURE.  And the coverage check keys on `${e%%:*}` -- the stem -- so the exemption is
1151:#  honoured throughout.  The teeth of the exempt arm depended on a format NOTHING VALIDATED.
1152:#
1153:#  THE FIX IS TO REFUSE, NOT TO GUESS.  A malformed entry is now RED and names itself; it is
1154:#  never silently treated as unexpired.  A `case` glob rather than a regex, because this runs
1155:#  under POSIX sh and `[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]` needs no external process.
1156:#
1157:#  ONE HELPER FOR BOTH MANIFESTS.  SM_EXEMPT and SC_EXEMPT carried byte-identical loops a few
1158:#  hundred lines apart, so a fix to one would have left the other -- which is this project's
1159:#  "grep for the siblings before calling it fixed" rule, and the reason both are converted
1160:#  here in one edit.
1161:exempt_expired () {          # exempt_expired <today> <entries...>
1162:    local today="$1"; shift
1163:    local out="" e d
1164:    for e in "$@"; do
1165:        case "$e" in
1166:            *:*) ;;
1167:            *) out="$out $e(NO-DATE)"; continue ;;
1168:        esac

1170:        case "$d" in
1171:            [0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]) ;;
1172:            *) out="$out ${e%%:*}(BAD-DATE:${d:-empty})"; continue ;;
1173:        esac

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command "@'
import ast, contextlib, copy, io, pathlib, re
p = pathlib.Path('tools/pipeline/check_probe_wiring.py').resolve()
src = p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
ns = {'__name__': 'adjudication_in_memory', '__file__': str(p)}
exec(compile(src, str(p), 'exec'), ns)
main_ast = next(n for n in ast.parse(src).body if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef) and n.name == 'main')
ns['gate_list'] = lambda rg: (['gate_arm.sh'], None)
ns['domain'] = lambda rg: (sorted(ns['MANIFEST']), 'synthetic-manifest')
ns['self_label'] = lambda path: None
ns['exec_sites'] = lambda rg, scripts: ({f: ['gate_arm.sh:synthetic'] for f, (cls, _) in ns['MANIFEST'].items() if cls == ns['CLASS_DETECTOR'] and f not in ns['EXEMPT']}, [], [])
original_exempt = dict(ns['EXEMPT'])
def check(op, today=None):
    tree = copy.deepcopy(main_ast)
    changed = 0
    for n in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(n, ast.Compare) and isinstance(n.left, ast.Name) and n.left.id == 'd' and len(n.comparators) == 1 and isinstance(n.comparators[0], ast.Name) and n.comparators[0].id == 'today':
            n.ops = [op()]
            changed += 1
    assert changed == 1
    exec(compile(ast.fix_missing_locations(ast.Module(body=[tree], type_ignores=[])), '<main-comparison-only>', 'exec'), ns)
    out = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(out):
        rc = ns['main']([] if today is None else ['--today', today])
    return rc, sum('EXPIRED EXEMPTION' in l for l in out.getvalue().splitlines())
print('Operational main, synthetic all-wired-or-exempt inventory; no tree scan or probe execution.')
print('exempt dates:', sorted(set(d for d, _ in original_exempt.values())))
for op in (ast.Lt, ast.LtE, ast.NotEq):
    print(op.__name__, [(day, check(op, day)) for day in ('2026-09-18', '2026-09-26', '2026-09-27', '2026-09-28')], 'default clock', check(op))
k = sorted(ns['EXEMPT'])[0]
ns['EXEMPT'][k] = ('2026-09-28', ns['EXEMPT'][k][1])
print('Split dates: earliest M5a', check(ast.Lt, '2026-09-27'), 'latest M5a', check(ast.Lt, '2026-09-28'))
ns['EXEMPT'] = dict(original_exempt)

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command "@'
import ast, pathlib, re
h = pathlib.Path('regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c').read_text()
start = h.index('} cases[] = {')
end = h.index('\\n\\t\\t};', start)
cases = re.findall(r'\"(["'^"'"\\n]+)\"', h[start:end])
label = 'R3 the reset still owes one interrupt'
exprs = {}
for n in ast.walk(ast.parse(pathlib.Path('regress/selfmutant.py').read_text())):
    if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and len(n.targets) == 1 and isinstance(n.targets[0], ast.Name) and n.targets[0].id in ('named', 'killed'):
        exprs[n.targets[0].id] = compile(ast.Expression(n.value), '<actual-helper-filter>', 'eval')
pin = '%-28s lowest value the counter ever held:' % cases[-1]
def table(good):
    lines = []
    for name in cases:
        lines.append('  %-4s %-44s %-28s rate 1 Hz peak 1 trough 1' % ('ok', 'R1 bounded across all 4 timers', name))
        lines.append('  %-4s %-44s %-28s peak 1 want 1' % ('ok', \"R5 the bound IS this timer's rate\", name))
        lines.append('  %-4s %-44s %-28s lowest value the counter ever held: %d' % ('ok' if good else 'FAIL', label, name, 1 if good else 0))
    lines.append('  FAIL R4 every case bounded (the selfmutant pin) 7 row failure(s) above')
    return '\\n'.join(lines)
def match(which, stdout):
    return eval(exprs[which], {'stdout': stdout, 'rowid': pin})
print('Synthetic row text using the C formats, checked by the actual helper filter AST.')
print('cases', len(cases), 'pin', repr(pin))
print('pristine unique matches', len(match('named', table(True))))
print('reset-to-zero mutant pinned kills', len(match('killed', table(False))))
print('R3 blinded + unrelated R4 red pinned kills', len(match('killed', table(True))))
print('R3 full prefix optional:', repr('%-44s %s' % (label, pin)))

GO-WITH-CHANGES — #536’s date repair is sound; #537 should take a unique R3 pin in-round.

**O1’s deferral is the disposition I would overturn.** Its cost premise is false: pinning R3 does not require another harness row or changing IDENTITY to 29/28. D1’s keep-red decision remains defensible, but “unreachable in the battery” is false. My own F5 also overstated what C2 actually asserts.

| F3 | **UPHOLD** — only the self-mutant debt is discharged; the drain-probe exemption remains. |
| F4 | **REFINE** — adopt the stamped-date test, but `!=` remains caught by M0 today; the lost coverage is an explicit, clock-independent day-before test. |
| F5 | **REFINE** — keeping the dependency disclosed and filing independence is reasonable, but C2 asserts `ABORT_OTHER`, not the particular PCI diagnostic. |

| F7 | **UPHOLD** — `gate` becomes the binary argument; the completion check still fails. |
| F8 | **REFINE** — the weakened verdict assertion accepts FAIL; retaining the anchored form is right. Harness mutation cannot validate that shell assertion, though a separate gate-assertion test could. |
| F9 | **UPHOLD** — `failures <= 1` weakens the pin, while the independent verdict still rejects one actual failure. |
| F10 | **UPHOLD** — latest and earliest coincide today; selecting latest makes M5a fail when exemption dates diverge. |
| F11 | **REFINE** — the default ROOT supplies absolute object paths; this is conditional on callers supplying an absolute ROOT when overriding it. |
| F12 | **REFINE** — the measurement settles compiler identity on this host, not the helper’s portability to hosts where `cc` differs from `gcc`. |

| F14 | **UPHOLD** — external build clearance can own freshness, provided the lane’s result is not presented as independently proving it. |

| O1 | **OVERTURN** — a case-qualified substring uniquely selects an existing R3 row; this can be fixed in the new wiring without adding rows. |
| O2 | **UPHOLD** — the rewritten/deleted passages correctly describe mk48’s new wiring status. |

| O6 | **CANNOT SETTLE** — 61 mk48 checks plus 32 hpcmips checks explains 93; clean-log provenance and checksum identity are outside the permitted artifact scope. |
| O7 | **UPHOLD** — recording the remaining wiring work respects the owner’s scope; the Alpha source-directory fixture writes are real preparation work for that round. |
| D1 | **REFINE** — keep red and coordinate both lanes plus sentinel accounting in `fbnobuild`; remove the claim that battery ordering makes the condition unreachable. |
| D2 | **UPHOLD** — none of the rejected alternatives supplies a necessary correction that overrides the owner’s stated scope. |

The narrower O1 repair follows directly from [the helper’s matcher](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/selfmutant.py:295): it searches the **whole output line**, including the case description. For example, this existing substring uniquely selects the slowest-legal R3 case:

```text
cats, slowest legal          lowest value the counter ever held:
```

I evaluated the actual helper matching expressions against synthetic rows using the C output formats: one pristine match, one mutant kill, and no pinned kill when R3 is blinded even though R4 is red. This establishes that the selector works; it is not a fresh detector execution.

“Not a census” justifies testing one R3 case. It does not justify substituting an aggregate for that selected assertion when the helper explicitly promises named-row protection. The remaining measurement to request is **an isolated helper run with this pin**, first normally and then with O1’s combined edits: expected OK, then FAIL because the selected R3 row no longer kills. No gate invocation is needed.

Several things remain missed or overstated:

- **The `.why` file contains another false explanation.** [Line 17](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/selfmutants/fbpending_bound.why:17) says a verdict-only assertion would accept reset-to-zero, immediately below a table showing `FBBOUND_VERDICT=FAIL`. A correct verdict assertion detects that failure; it cannot establish *which row* detected it. Its subsequent claim that neither repeated row can be pinned also overlooks case-qualified matching. Correct both with the new pin.

- **D1’s reachability argument fails by inspection.** [run.sh](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/run.sh:82) continues after a failed or skipped gate 1, and selectors can omit gate 1. Keep-red is defensible as incomplete apparatus handling pending a coordinated repair—not because this state is unreachable.

- **O5’s “ONLY thing” claim overlooks existing checks.** An absent main-lane log already fails sentinel accounting; the stale-log case is the dangerous exception. Also, [SC_EXEMPT](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_offline.sh:2223) still contains two **2026-09-02** deadlines. On September 18, those independently redden a run reaching that check. This is pre-existing and separate from #536’s eleven exemptions.

- **Calendar validity remains unchecked.** `DATE_RE` accepts `2026-09-99`. Running the actual checker logic with synthetic wiring data and one such later exemption produced PASS today and on M5a’s date; M5b failed through the other ten exemptions. Thus the pair does not validate every deadline. Conversely, an impossible date selected as the minimum makes the new `fromisoformat()` raise. File calendar validation separately; the actual eleven valid dates do not trigger it.

Two section-4 explanations need refinement:

**C2’s new comment overstates its specificity.** [The assertion](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/hpcmips_ctor_probe.py:522) checks only `cls == ABORT_OTHER`. Another unrelated SIGABRT would satisfy it after the PCI defect was fixed. Say: “C2 currently obtains its unrelated-abort control from mvme1600’s PCI construction defect; successful construction will turn C2 red.” That accurately discloses the dependency without claiming diagnostic attribution.

**The M5a change is right, but its rationale should distinguish pair coverage from suite coverage.** With today’s date and table, `!=` produces eleven expired exemptions and fails M0. Moving M5a therefore does **not currently lose suite detection of `!=`**. It does lose deterministic day-before coverage, which matters when the real date equals the stamp. The nearby pre-existing claim that the comparison is tested “exhaustively” should also be narrowed. Likewise, [gate_offline’s “RED on that date”](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_offline.sh:1081) should say **after** that date: its comparison is also strict `<`.

I confirmed HEAD `3c41f01`, isolated the relevant hunks from unrelated working-tree changes, and used only read-only inspection and in-memory checks. No gates, battery, artifacts, or ledger were read or executed.
